Um... I've been trying to keep up with my hobbies, I really have. I don't want to just sit around and do nothing. But... I guess I haven't been enjoying them very much lately. 

I used to love planning hikes for the weekends, and I'd take all these photos... I also used to do a lot of cooking on Sundays, and I even volunteered at the local library. I always liked being helpful. But now... um, it's hard to explain. It's like all those things just feel like... items on a checklist? Um... yeah, I guess it is most of the day. Like I was saying before, I really do try to stay active, but... it's just so hard to find the energy for it now.

Um, yes... I suppose I have. I really try not to let it show, because I know everyone gets tired and I don't want to be a complainer, but... well, even after I spend a full night in bed, my body just feels so heavy. It honestly feels like I’ve been up closing an audit for three days straight.

Um, well... I guess I have lost a little bit of weight, but I promise it's not because I'm doing it on purpose or trying to be unhealthy or anything.

Um... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe... about seven months? 

It kind of started around the time my husband stopped working... his company had some downsizing, and, well, things just got a bit stressful. I really tried to keep everything running smoothly at home, you know, being supportive and keeping my own routine going, but... I guess that's when I stopped wanting to do my photography or go on those hikes. 

I feel bad even connecting the two things. I mean, he's the one who lost his job, so I should be the strong one, right? I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm blaming him or making it about me. I just... I think the stress of it all kind of caught up with me. Is it normal for something like that to make you lose interest in your own life for this long? I really am trying to get back to my old self.

I just didn't want to be a bother if it was something I could fix on my own. I guess I haven't been enjoying them very much lately. \n\nI used to love planning hikes for the weekends, and I'd take all these photos... Even when I'm right in the middle of doing something I used to love, I just feel sort of empty. I mean, is it normal to feel so disconnected from things you're supposed to like?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no. No, I haven't. And I just don't seem to care enough to even start them anymore. \n\nI feel terrible saying that, because I know I should be grateful I have the free time to do those things. I just... I don't know why I can't seem to enjoy them right now. Is it bad that I feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel pleasure when you do manage to start one of your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess sometimes I do? A little bit? I mean, I really try to find the good in it once I get going. Like, if I force myself to make dinner on a Sunday, I try to tell myself how nice it is that I'm providing a good meal. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound ungrateful... it mostly just feels flat. Even when I'm right in the middle of doing something I used to love, I just feel sort of empty. Like I'm going through the motions just so I don't let anyone down. \n\nI really wish I could just snap out of it and feel happy about it again. Does that... I mean, is it normal to feel so disconnected from things you're supposed to like?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no. No, I haven't. I mean, I know I've been feeling really down, but I would never... I really couldn't do that to my husband, especially not with him being out of work right now. I just want to get back to normal so I can be supportive of him again. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm wasting your time by coming in, since I'm not... you know, in that kind of extreme danger? I just really want to be a good partner and do whatever you think is best to fix this. Is it okay that I'm here even without feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a loss of interest in your hobbies most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it is most of the day. Like I was saying before, I really do try to stay active, but... it's just so hard to find the energy for it now. \n\nI used to be so organized with my weekends—planning out these big hikes, bringing my camera to take photos, doing all my meal prep and cooking on Sundays. And my volunteer shifts at the library... I always looked forward to them. I really liked feeling useful. \n\nBut lately, pretty much all day, every day... those things just feel like, I don't know, chores? Like items on a checklist that I just... I don't care enough to even start. I feel so lazy admitting that. I really should be doing more, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think I'm just giving up... I really am trying to push through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a significant decrease in energy levels most days?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I suppose I have. I really try not to let it show, because I know everyone gets tired and I don't want to be a complainer, but... well, even after I spend a full night in bed, my body just feels so heavy. I feel terrible even saying that out loud. I mean, I know I should be able to handle basic daily tasks without needing a break. I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses or being lazy. Is it normal to feel that exhausted when you haven't even really done anything?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... It almost feels like the day is already lost before I've even managed to get out of bed. I always try to push past it and put on a smile, I promise, I just... it's been hard lately. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe... about seven months? \n\nIt sort of started right around the time my husband, um, stopped working. There was some downsizing at his company, which I know happens to a lot of people, so I really shouldn't let it get to me this much. I've been trying my best to just keep everything running smoothly and not make a big fuss about it. \n\nI hope it's okay that I waited this long to come in... I just didn't want to be a bother if it was something I could fix on my own. Does that sound like a normal amount of time to feel this way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do lately?\n\nPatient: Um... I've been trying to keep up with my hobbies, I really have. I don't want to just sit around and do nothing. But... I guess I haven't been enjoying them very much lately. \n\nI used to love planning hikes for the weekends, and I'd take all these photos... I also used to do a lot of cooking on Sundays, and I even volunteered at the local library. I always liked being helpful. But now... um, it's hard to explain. And I just don't seem to care enough to even start them anymore. \n\nI feel terrible saying that, because I know I should be grateful I have the free time to do those things. I just... I don't know why I can't seem to enjoy them right now. Is it bad that I feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel pleasure when you do manage to start one of your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess sometimes I do? A little bit? I mean, I really try to find the good in it once I get going. Like, if I force myself to make dinner on a Sunday, I try to tell myself how nice it is that I'm providing a good meal. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound ungrateful... it mostly just feels flat. Even when I'm right in the middle of doing something I used to love, I just feel sort of empty. Like I'm going through the motions just so I don't let anyone down. \n\nI really wish I could just snap out of it and feel happy about it again. Does that... I mean, is it normal to feel so disconnected from things you're supposed to like?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no. No, I haven't. I just want to get back to normal so I can be supportive of him again. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm wasting your time by coming in, since I'm not... you know, in that kind of extreme danger? I really am trying to push through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a significant decrease in energy levels most days?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I suppose I have. Is it normal to feel that exhausted when you haven't even really done anything?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have lost a little bit of weight, but I promise it's not because I'm doing it on purpose or trying to be unhealthy or anything. \n\nIt's just that... well, eating feels like such a chore right now. I know I need to eat, so I really do try to force something down when I can, just to keep going. But I just don't have an appetite at all. I noticed the other day that my work skirts are getting pretty loose, and I haven't been working out or anything. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm neglecting myself... I really am trying to make sure I eat. I know nutrition is important. Is it okay that I'm struggling with that a little bit? I'm trying to do better, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt a loss of interest in your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe... about seven months? \n\nIt kind of started around the time my husband stopped working... his company had some downsizing, and, well, things just got a bit stressful. I really tried to keep everything running smoothly at home, you know, being supportive and keeping my own routine going, but... I really am trying to get back to my old self.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death, even if they are not about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I know it's common for people feeling like this to maybe have those kinds of thoughts, but I haven't. \n\nI've just been so focused on trying to keep things together for my husband and making sure we stay afloat with my job.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I know we just talked about me feeling a little sluggish sometimes, but... restless? No, I wouldn't say that. I really try to stay composed, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who's constantly fidgeting or pacing around the office making everyone else nervous. 

I mean, I sit at a desk all day, so it's important to just stay put and be professional. Even when I'm feeling that heavy feeling, I just stay in my chair and try to look like I'm working hard. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was anxious or couldn't handle the pressure of the audits. 

So, no, definitely not restless. I think I'm doing a pretty good job of keeping still and not bothering anyone. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just staying quiet and out of the way?

I don't care enough to even start. I feel so lazy admitting that. I really should be doing more, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think I'm just giving up... I really am trying to push through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a significant decrease in energy levels most days?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I suppose I have. I really try not to let it show, because I know everyone gets tired and I don't want to be a complainer, but... well, even after I spend a full night in bed, my body just feels so heavy. It honestly feels like I’ve been up closing an audit for three days straight. \n\nAnd then just doing the simplest things, like taking a shower or trying to sort through some basic insurance paperwork... it just completely wipes me out. I feel terrible even saying that out loud. I mean, I know I should be able to handle basic daily tasks without needing a break. I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses or being lazy. Is it normal to feel that exhausted when you haven't even really done anything?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I noticed the other day that my work skirts are getting pretty loose, and I haven't been working out or anything. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm neglecting myself... I really am trying to make sure I eat. I know nutrition is important. Is it okay that I'm struggling with that a little bit? I'm trying to do better, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt a loss of interest in your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe... about seven months? \n\nIt kind of started around the time my husband stopped working... his company had some downsizing, and, well, things just got a bit stressful. I really tried to keep everything running smoothly at home, you know, being supportive and keeping my own routine going, but... I guess that's when I stopped wanting to do my photography or go on those hikes. \n\nI feel bad even connecting the two things. I really am trying to get back to my old self.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death, even if they are not about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need to help me. I'm doing okay in that regard, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of sadness and loss of interest nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so, yeah. It feels like it's been pretty much every day for a while now. \n\nI mean, I really try to put on a brave face, especially at work and for my husband. I don't want to drag anyone else down with me, you know? So I try to smile and get my work done and keep the house tidy. Does moderate sound about right for what I've been describing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been withdrawing from social interactions or feeling like you're a burden to friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've been doing that. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get past this, and I hope making the effort to stay social is a good sign.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or making decisions at work or in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. Maybe it takes a tiny bit more effort to get started on a task sometimes, just because of that heavy feeling I mentioned, but once I'm doing it, I'm fine.
